Report · page 16

On top for sixth consecutive year

Bill Laws

British Athletics League Division One

Match 4, Wigan, 21 August 2002.

1 Woodford Green with EL 340; 2 Birchfield H 326; 3 Belgrave H 312.3; 4 Trafford AC 310.3; 5 Shaftesbury Barnet H 261.3; 6 Harrow AC 226; 7 Newham & Essex Beagles 214; 8 Border H & AC 164.

The Prologue

With a clear 6 points lead over Woodford Green coming in to this match we would have been forgiven for taking a relaxed view of this - the deciding fixture - which could determine Belgrave's place in the record books. Winning the premier men's title for a sixth consecutive time was our aim and equalling Wolves' eight wins all-told, and the team managers once again approached selection with the thoroughness expected by all concerned. Hotel bookings were made and letters and maps sent out to over 50 A team potential participants. And then the effects of a long hard season and the prospect of a long trip to windy Wigan became apparent as the responses fed back. Thirteen reported injuries, six were already planning to be on holiday and eleven more couldn't make it due to work and other commitments.

Desperate phone calls and the Friday tally showed we would be approaching this match with the smallest ever Belgrave squad to grace a BAL Div 1 track.

The Magnificent SEVENteen

And it was down to just seventeen of our stalwarts who turned up on the day to support the cause. And not all of them were fit. Kevin Brown was driven to the match with a trapped nerve in his back. His normal 54m+ effort became 12m less but his brave effort was typical of the fighting spirit as everyone mixed and matched to cover most events - but inevitably there were gaps. There were many unusual sights. Stuart Faben in the hammer and shot, Andrew Bennett in the high hurdles and high jump backed by James Tuson in the long, triple and high. Ben Flint won the pole vault from a high class field but unfortunately Paul Williamson no heighted in difficult swirling wind conditions.

Then on the track sprinters Darren Chin and Graham Beasley cleaned up with max points in both sprints. Graham handed out decisive defeats to Duaine Ladejo of Birchfield who by choice ran in the B100 and A200 against our man. Darren and Graham were joined by Matt Douglas and Femi Daramola to also win the sprint relay. Femi had a poor long jump but made amends with a season's best in the triple.

Meanwhile the middle distance squad performed magnificently. Chris Moss fresh (almost) from a fine 800 in Sweden won the B 800 and A 1500 while Neal Speaight answered a last minute call-up from the team managers and won the A 800. He also finished a creditable third in the B 400. We had not one of our 400m specialists in the squad and Matt Douglas stepped in for the A 400 leaving Andrew Bennett as our sole 400 hurdler. In cold and windy conditions times were irrelevant. Andrew finished third having paced himself to perfection.

But there was no better example of the team's determination to prove their quality than Will Cockerell's fine PB in the 5000m. Not content with that, just thirty minutes later Will stepped to the line in the 3000 steelechase with 18 year old William Docherty who was making his debut in our British League squad. And this time Will Docherty ran a 2sec PB with Will Cockerell again adding points to our growing tally.

Fortunately England and Belgrave team physio Paula Wild was working flat out keeping the team in one piece throughout the afternoon but even she couldn't present us with enough fit runners for the long relay which we declined to run! But by then we were assured of third spot on the day to ensure a wonderful League win. Yes, six years in a row Belgrave have been GB Men's Champions and one more win will give us the clear record for the most wins all told.

Thanks

And thanks to everybody who supported us this year. None more so than the men who competed in all four matches despite a full competitive schedule. They are eight in number and are: Graham Beasley, Andrew Bennett, Kevin Brown, Will Cockerell, Femi Daramola, Matt Douglas, Stuart Faben and Ben Flint.

Bill Laws

In 2002 Lee Newman announced his retirement at the tender age of 29 years due to recurring injuries. Many thought Lee would be the man to de-throne Abi Ekoku as the most prolific scorer for Belgrave in British Athletic League competitions and this prompted us to bring the All Time Rankings up to date after a 3 year gap.

To do justice to the ever increasing number of impressive scorers we have extended the list to the top 70 and in addition have calculated the current standing of many of our most recent and current team members. The number of match appearances are shown in brackets.

Report · page 18

Bels battle to 6th in Lisbon

European Champion Clubs Cup 2002

Hopes of an improvement on last year's 5th place took a knock as the team gathered at Gatwick and found that Dwain Chambers would not be accompanying them on the trip to Lisbon. Although desperate for races, a niggle at this stage of a crucial season meant that our key sprinter was unavailable.

A further complication was revealed at the pre-match technical meeting when it was announced that a second Portuguese team would be taking part, pushing the number of competing teams up to nine even though only eight lanes were available in the Estádio Universitário de Lisboa. The shortage of lanes would be overcome by splitting the sprints and hurdles into two races, putting the slower runners into a race of four men which would be followed by the faster five men in race two. Not only did this destroy the shorter events as a spectacle, it made team positions difficult to calculate immediately - and in at least one case affected the result.

DAY ONE

Hammer - Mike Jones. Drama struck early when on his first attempt and into his fourth turn, Mike Jones suddenly lost control. As the 16lb hammer took command, the Belgrave man was thrown violently to the ground ... and, worryingly, he did not move a muscle as he lay face down in the throwing area. Officials and spectators were similarly frozen until the spell was broken after what seemed an eternity and help in the form of a worried Team Manager charged towards the prostrate figure. Slowly Mike came around and his first words were: "How far did it go? How long have I been here?"

Suffering from a bout of tonsilitis, he had lost spacial awareness as his spinning gained momentum. At three turns and going into four he thought to himself, "this is going to go far," but wasn't quite sure where he was. The next thing he knew was that he was opening his eyes and closely studying the tartan surface around the hammer circle.

Mike was badly bruised and had hit his head on the circle rim but having hauled himself back onto his feet, a short jog out onto the grass convinced him that he was going to continue - and the competition recommenced. A courageous second round attempt moved him into third place but as others consolidated in rounds three and four Mick, not surprisingly, had trouble staying in the circle and was unable to improve. His normal 70 metres plus should have given him 3rd or even 2nd - but the team were lucky to get any points at all, for a man any less valiant would have called it a day after that first attempt.

1 V.Khersontsev (RUS) 76.85; 2 P.Sedlacek (CZE) 73.08; 3 V.Costa (POR) 69.95; 4 M.Campeny (ESP) 68.34; 5 M.Jones (GBR) 66.85 (x, 66.85, x, x); 6 A.Vitel E Silva (POR II) 64.95; 7 E.Marioni (ITA) 64.69; 8 W.Ciofani (FRA) 60:32; 9 I.Giller (ISR) 48:87. After 1 event: 1 SC Luch (RUS) 9; 2 Dukla Praha (CZE) 8; 3 Sporting Club Portugal (POR) 7, 4 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 (ESP) 6; 5 Belgrave H (GBR) 5; 6 Futebol Clube Porto (POR) 4; 7 VB Carabinieri (ITA) 3; 8 CA Montreuil (FRA) 2; 9 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 1.

400 metres hurdles - Matt Douglas. A gaunt looking Matt drove hard from the outside lane in the faster of the two races. Laid low a while back by a bug which had caused him to lose half a stone, Matt was not yet back to his best but this race was an important step on the road to sub-50. Clearly ahead at 200 metres Matt was still fighting the Russian Boris Gorban for the lead halfway around the final turn, but into the straight his less than perfect condition began to tell and as he began to tread water the Czech and Portuguese runners squeezed past. Just one man in race one beat his time and it was another 5 points in the bag with the team moving up into 4th.

Race 1. 1 G.Carabelli (ITA) 50.37; 2 P.Rodrigues (POR II) 51.68; 3 M.Adamopoulos (ISR) 52.32; 4 X.Ravanet (FRA) 52.74. Race 2. 1 B.Gorban (RUS) 49.27; 2 S.Tesarik (CZE) 50.03; 3 C.Silva (POR) 50.28; 4 M.Douglas (GBR) 50.82; 5 C.Navarro (ESP) 52.30. After two events: 1 SC Luch 18; 2 Dukla Praha 16; 3 Sporting Club Portugal 14, 4 Belgrave H 10; 5 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 9; 6 VB Caribinieri 9; 7 Futebol Clube Porto 8; 8 CA Montreuil 3; 9 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 3.

100 metres - Marlon Dickson. Marlon has been with us a long time. It's a dozen years now since we first saw him racing in the Thameside League - and while, in far off Battersea, another Thameside was drawing to a close, Marlon lined up with some very tasty sprinters in Portugal. Drawn in the slower of the two races Marlon was neck and neck with Sunday Emmanuel, representing Prague but hailing from Nigeria. Just in the final couple of paces half a metre of daylight opened up and Marlon was 2nd. Race 2 saw the "Russian" Brian Lewis hold off the "Portuguese" Francis Obikwelu but Marlon's time - a pb if it hadn't been for the following wind - stood up well and it was 5th and 5 points yet again.

Race 1. (4.7) 1 S.Emmanuel (CZE) 10.43; 2 M.Dickson (GBR) 10.50; 3 T Kafri (ISR) 10.60; 4 M.Barbosa (POR II) 10.87. Race 2. (3.6) 1 B.Lewis (RUS) 10.11; 2 F.Obikwelu (POR) 10.23; 3 M.Diarra (FRA) 10.41; 4 A.Rodriguez (ESP) 10.55; 5 A.Rabino (ITA) 10.64. After 3 events: 1 SC Luch 27; 2 Dukla Praha 22; 3 Sporting Club Portugal 22, 4 Belgrave H 15; 5 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 13; 6 VB Caribinieri 11; 7 CA Montreuil 10; 8 Futebol Clube Porto 9; 9 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 6.

1500 metres - Andy Graffin. Before the runners had cleared the back straight for the first time one could see that this was going to be a real "championship" race with everyone relying on a final kick. After a lap in 67 Andy was handily placed near the front and at 700m (1:59) he had moved right onto the shoulder of the leader. With two laps taking 2:15 the Belgrave man had had enough and down the back straight he ran ahead and injected some serious speed – but could he break away? Andy's third lap took 56 seconds but there was still a queue of kickers lined up behind the claret and gold vest. Entering the finishing straight the line astern formation became line abreast and the race could have been anyone's. As target man and pacer for the previous half mile Andy suffered most and in spite of moving at almost the same pace as everyone else, he slipped back to 6th as a mere 63/100ths covered the first half dozen home. It was a Caribinieri vest that was first across the line, and for the first time in the meeting the Russians failed to take maximum points.

1 C.Obrist (ITA) 3:51.17; 2 M.Sneberger (CZE) 3:51.30; 3 V.Shabunin (RUS) 3:51.33; 4 P.Esteso (ESP) 3:51.47; 5 A.Bispo (POR) 3:51.76; 6 A.Graffin (GBR) 3:51.80; 7 Y.Gerachiw (ISR) 3:54.71; 8 S.Benfares (FRA) 3:55.49; 9 A.Alheira (POR II) 3:57.13. After 4 events: 1 SC Luch 34; 2 Dukla Praha 30; 3 Sporting Club Portugal 27, 4 VB Caribinieri 20; 5 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 19; 6 Belgrave H 19; 7 CA Montreuil 12; 8 Futebol Clube Porto 10; 9 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 9.

400 metres - Sean Baldock. Nursing a knee problem, Sean's chances of racing a hard 400 were rated as only 70% a week before the race. But the niggle improved to such an extent that our man was quite confident that everything would be ok - and it was. It was his first race of the year and rather than take out the first 200 in the low 21s he took it a little steadier in something more like 22. The result was a very satisfying 2nd place and the knowledge that some fast times are just around the corner. Sean's record in the ECCC is probably as good as anyone's - four wins and a 2nd place.

Race 1. 1 A.Morante (ESP) 47.59; 2 R.Loubli (FRA) 48.10; 3 V.Moreno (POR) 48.15; 4 N.Green (ISR) 48.51. Race 2. 1 A.Semenov (RUS) 46.66; 2 S.Baldock (GBR) 46.79; 3 K.Blaha (CZE) 46.82; 4 M.Salvucci (ITA) 47.61; A.Pinto (POR II) dnf. After 5 events: 1 SC Luch 43; 2 Dukla Praha 37; 3 Sporting Club Portugal 30, 4 Belgrave H 27; 5 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 25; 6 VB Caribinieri 25; 7 CA Montreuil 16; 8 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 11; 9 Futebol Clube Porto 10.

High Jump – Ben Challenger. Ben has had some highs (World Student Games and European Under 23 winner) and lows in the last few years - but put a Belgrave vest on him and he always provides the goods. First time clearances at 2.05 and 2.09 gave him the confidence to pass at 2.13. Taking 2.16 at the second attempt and with four men left in the hunt, he then elected to give 2.19 a miss as well. Rybakov and Janku both took this height at their second trial while the Spaniard went out. Three men were left in - including big Ben. Both his opponents sailed over 2.21 at the first time of asking while Ben could go no further but 3rd place was more than we expected and the team moved into third spot at the expense of Sporting Club who picked up a single point in this discipline.

1 T.Janku (CZE) 2.25; 2 Y.Rybakov (RUS) 2.23; 3 B.Challenger (GBR) 2.16 (2.05 o, 2.09 o, 2.13 -, 2.16 xo, 2.19 -, 2.21 xxx); 4 J.Bermejo (ESP) 2.16; 5 N.Ciotti (ITA) 2.13; 6 C.Matusevich (ISR) 2.09; 7 M.Raifak (FRA) 2.05; 8 R.Goncalves (POR II) 2.05; 9 J.Mattes (POR) 2.05. After 6 events: 1 SC Luch 51; 2 Dukla Praha 46; 3 Belgrave H 34; 4 Puma Chapin Jerez 2002 31; 5 Sporting Club Portugal 31, 6 VB Caribinieri 30; 7 CA Montreuil 19; 8 Maccabi Tel-Aviv (ISR) 15; 9 Futebol Clube Porto 12.

Long Jump - Darren Thompson. Where there are swings there are usually roundabouts and yearned for for points in the long jump to fortify our team position didn't materialise. We've seen some monster marginal fouls from Darren Thompson in the past and every one of his efforts had our supporters lifting with him as he hit the board. Two "no jumps" were followed by a 7.23m effort that at least warranted a point and while in the last round the leader Danil Burkenya celebrated with a mighty 8.35m to wrap it up and take the Cup record, Darren could only muster another 7.23. He was initially placed equal 7th, sharing with the Israeli, until a sharp eyed Sir Eddie reminded the scorers that the Belgrave man had a superior second best effort and 1.5 team points were turned into 2.

5000 metres - Spencer Barden. A fine 13:48 at Loughborough a week earlier had given Spencer a lot of confidence but with a few laps remaining, a sudden break left our man adrift and the blustery evening wind made it very difficult to regain contact. Alvarez, an 8:22 steeplechaser, was similarly caught on the hop but Spen's competitive finish made sure that once again a Belgrave singlet crossed the line in our favourite position – 5th.

Shot - Greg Beard. Nineteen year-old Greg found himself in a hot competition and no mistake. Three throws over 19 metres were registered in the opening round with Viratyuk heading the field at 19.53m. A fourth man approached 20m in round two and then the penultimate round saw Italian Dal Soglio go ahead with 19.80m. In the face of all this Greg, up an age-group and up an implement weight since last season, was faced with a daunting task. He improved in round two but was unable to make a mark in the last two rounds while Viratyuk let out a huge bellow to celebrate breaching the 20m barrier and going back into the lead. Greg vows that next year he'll be back and will be taking a few scalps!

4 x 100 metres relay - Graham Beasley, Darren Chinn, Marlon Dickson, Matt Douglas. It was not an exhilarating weekend for Darren Chin. Inexplicably left out of the non-scoring 100m by the organisers when his name was the first to be registered, he was left with a relay run which went close to giving us no score at all. Graham Beasley ran a good first turn but he and Darren then had great difficulty in connecting and by the time the second Belgrave man had the baton it was a lost cause. Twenty metres adrift along the back straight, our team gradually halved the deficit to the next team ahead, but not only were we 5th in the "A" race, we were slower than each of the teams in the "B" relay and a measly single point was all we could add to the halfway total.

With the second Portuguese team not involved in the relegation stakes we were now looking over our shoulders at CA Montreuil. If they should catch us on day two it would be Group B for GBR in 2003!

DAY TWO

110 metres hurdles - Neil Owen. Neil immediately made up 2.5 points on our Spanish rivals and was close to gaining more when his desperate final lunge in the sprint hurdles just failed to catch another man on the line. The competition had been even tighter in the previous race where de los Santos and Adamopoulos could not be split by the electric timing and both recorded the same time to the 1/1000th of a second. The half point gained here by the Spanish club proved to be crucial at the end of the match. CA Montreuil provided the slowest hurdler and we began to feel a lot more comfortable about avoiding the relegation zone.

In Madrid last year he headed Kozhvnikov only for the verdict to go against him on the line as the Russian managed a late charge to clock the same time. Girls at the Games - Commonwealth Games 2002

Above: Representing Wales, Emma Davies reduced the Belgrave best for 800 metres twice at Manchester City Stadium. She was placed 4th in her heat with 2:04.2 and then clocked 2:03.93 to run to 6th in her semi-final.

Above right: Setting new Belgrave standards in all that she does, Shelley Newman became Belgrave's first ever female medallist at a Commonwealth Games, taking bronze in the discus with 58m 13cm. Husband Lee was 11th in the shot and 8th in the discus.

Below: Heptathlete Julie Hollman set a tremendous score of over 6000 points in the Multistars Meet at Desenzano del Garda, Italy, in May but was beset by problems when the major Games came around later in the year and unable to reproduce her best form.

Right: Catherine Berry set a club best for 5000 metres at the trial, placed 10th in the Games, and improved her best again at the Crystal Palace IAAF Grand Prix. Latest news is that on May 17th 2003 at Gresham, Oregon, she improved yet again to 15:30:25.

Obituary · page 22

Gordon Doubleday 1912-2001

Eric Hall

Obituaries

Gordon Doubleday 1912-2001

Gordon, who died 15th June 2001, aged 89, is best described as one of the great all-rounders of Belgrave. He started by running in a novice race in 1934, went on to be a more than useful Club runner over road and country; converted to walking to very good effect; played a decisive role in the administration of the Club; was President for 1969/70; a fount of all knowledge on things Belgravian, and was in great demand by other bodies such as the Centurions and the Road Runners Club.

Above all this quiet man enjoyed his athletics, was proud of his Club, and was a regular contributor to our club magazine as well as serving a spell as Editor. A useful man in a team when at his fittest he will be best remembered for the part he played in the team which won the "Junior" National Walk in 1939 and the several occasions when he was the key fourth scorer in Surrey County Championships which were, usually two horse races between ourselves and Surrey WC. "Doub" could be relied on to know what was required and then to deliver the placing - on occasion due to the absence of our stars.

His spell as Hon Treasurer, from 1950 to 1952, was after a wartime move to Exeter where he became involved with the local club. Life Membership was bestowed on him in 1952. Among his other attainments was the Club Honours badge for his marathon performances and his membership of the Centurions that exclusive body of people who have walked 100 miles in 24 hours, which he accomplished in the London to Brighton and back race in 1947.

In later years Gordon retired to just outside Bath and typically became involved with local Clubs and events while, until ill health prevented it, he would return to Wimbledon for our more important fixtures as a timekeeper or recorder. On these occasions he was ever ready with recollections of races he had seen, and athletes he had known. A fund of tales which he always related in a quiet manner. In addition he kept in touch with a wide rage of members and was always keen to hear the latest news or answer our queries.

It is only fitting to end this tribute with the words written by the late Alf Harley in the Belgravian of December 1969, ending his description of "Doub" as the new President "All this, plus his pleasing personality, his ability to enthuse, and his great loyalty to Belgrave make us fortunate indeed in our selection of President." A tribute those who knew him would all endorse.

Eric Hall

Gordon DoubledayGordonDoubAlf HarleyEric Hall

Obituary · page 22

Les Stokell 1919-2001

Eric Herbert

Les Stokell passed away at Sidmouth in Devon on 20 January 2001 in his 82nd year. He was an active club member in the early post-war years, having joined the Club at the age of 27 in 1946. He ran in successful Belgrave teams in the Southern Counties and National Cross Country Championships and the London to Brighton Relays, as well as in the South of Thames race one year when he came in 7th. During a spell in Canada he broke the Canadian 10 mile record on the road in 48 minutes 45 seconds. The course was measured afterwards to confirm the accuracy. He was contemporary with Arthur Penny, Bert Footer, Vic Blowfield, Ted Stimpson, Charlie Smart, Harry Shields, Len Marchant, Bill Lucas, my brother Len and many others. They were good years for Belgrave.

A professional soldier before and during the War, Les became a Sergeant Major in the Royal Marines and was involved in the storming of southern Sicily at Anzio before the beach-heads were secured. He also took part in the post D-Day landings at Walcheren on the Netherlands coast, finishing up with the loss of two fingers and a shattered shin to his left leg. He took part in other actions and was lucky to "make it".

To put it mildly, Les was a hero, but he would never agree to that. He was a most modest and matter-of-fact chap with a fund of stories to tell in his dry humorous way.

Charles Leslie Stokell grew up in Eltham with his two brothers and three sisters. His exploits with his childhood "gang" were comparable with those of William Brown of Just William fame and he would often regale his family with these stories and have them crying with laughter.

So much can be said of Charles, the adult, affectionately known as Les (or "blockhead" if you were one of his pupils). He was an extraordinary and unique man, a man's man, a non-conformist, some might even say eccentric - half a step out with the world but ever confident that it was they who were out of step with him.

He was undoubtedly a very brave man. He signed up for the Royal Marine Commandos and fought all over Europe and in the Atlantic from 1939-1945. Notably he took up his part in D-Day after shinning down a drainpipe at a hospital, "discharging" himself after yet another major injury, to ensure he would be with his comrades.

The loyalty and respect he inspired during his time with Royal Marine 41 Commando is legendary, as are his own actions of extreme heroism. "The best days of my life" he would continue to say right up to the end.

Amongst his awards was the Military Medal, awarded for outstanding courage in the field, but it was typical of Les that he would never disclose, when badgered by his children, why he was given it, telling them that everyone who was there got one. Friends and family were rarely given true accounts of the action he had seen unless it gave rise to a funny story. But those who were there and are still alive today know the truth and bear witness to his remarkable courage.

From his earliest involvement as a Physical Training Instructor in the Royal Marines, he became a first class national and international athlete and sportsman. He qualified as a teacher specialising in games and later, also, mathematics. An interest in antiques first had him running a Sunday stall in Petticoat Lane, then a shop, Mr. Bumble's, and finally setting up his own antique market with Dot in the village of Henfield, West Sussex. Nothing in his home was considered safe from the stock list, even if nailed down and his family learned to lock away their treasured possessions from a very early age.

So many people knew and loved Les. A fellow teacher from Archbishop Tenison's, where he spent the major part of his teaching career, said colleagues would have gone through fire and water for Les. They considered it a privilege to have known him. Not a man to suffer fools gladly, but one who would always open his heart, (which was large) to anyone who genuinely wanted to learn. He had a wonderfully dry sense of humour but was never able to tell a funny story without the give-away creasing up of one eye.

We pay tribute to a rare individual, a man with a fierce indomitable spirit. We pay tribute to Les Stokell. May he now rest peacefully.

Eric Herbert

Les StokellLesCharles Leslie StokellCharlesArthur PennyBert FooterVic BlowfieldTed StimpsonCharlie SmartHarry ShieldsLen MarchantBill LucasLenDotEric Herbert

Obituary · page 23

Bill Couzens 1936-2002

George Flanagan

Obituaries

Bill Couzens 1936-2002

Complications arose when Bill went to work at Rosser & Russell where his father worked and was also known as 'Bill'. So our Bill used his middle name of Jim and this stayed with him for all his professional working associations. Bill met his 'wife to be' Gwen at work so she also knew him as Jim. I met Bill at the age of thirteen when we started together at Christopher Wren School.

Evidence of Bill's competitiveness, his perseverance and doggedness was seen early on when he entered the school boxing championships and put up an extremely good performance against the best in the school who was taller with a longer reach and later frequented fairgrounds to battle for prize money. Bill and I enjoyed many camping weekends together at Ashtead Forest, Walton on Thames, the river Mole and many others. He also enjoyed the 3rd Fulham scout camps and made many friends. It was at this time Bill started his life-long interest in fishing.

On leaving school at 15 years he started an apprenticeship with Rosser & Russell to train as a heating and ventilating engineer.

Bill joined Belgrave Harriers at 16 years of age – more about this later. Music of many types has always played a large part in Bill's life. I remember the times when, at Baron's Court at the Couzens' family home, Bill, George Stanley, Pam's 'husband to be' and myself would drive his parents and sisters Pam and Sylvia crazy with our attempts to play skiffle, imitating Lonnie Donegan. Many years later at Belgrave Hall a much-improved group was formed with Bill on guitar and this group was affectionately known as the 'Swinging Jockstraps'. Bill did his National service at 20 years of age, joining the army and representing them extensively in athletics.

Gwen and Bill were married in 1961 and their family was completed with the arrival of Tina and David. Years of family happiness followed of which many people would be envious.

Bill wanted a dog when Tina and David were young but he wanted one that was good with children so he got a Great Dane that he called Jason. The dog was huge and when it laid down in the lounge it more resembled a horse. At this time Bill had a small boat on the Thames and Jason always wanted to be with the family and was sad if left behind – but he was terrified of water. Bill always had the dilemma of whether to leave Jason behind or make him walk the plank each time in a nervous state. Jason nearly always went with them! For nearly twenty years Bill and Gwen with John and Jenny Martin have held together a camp over the August bank holiday in the New Forest. This has always been well supported by Tina, Tony and the children, David and Stephanie, along with other members of the family and many friends. There was one occasion when there were at least thirty people present.

Bill and Don MacLean inaugurated the Denmark Pot Darts championship as far back as 1963 and Bill has had an enviable eleven wins.

One extremely amusing incident occurred when Bill was on holiday on the Norfolk Broads. He attempted to take a rather large cabin cruiser under Potter Heigham Bridge. There was not quite enough headroom and the boat became wedged under the bridge. It happened alongside a pub garden where there were plenty of onlookers. So Bill quickly invited them aboard, this lowered the boat in the water enough for it to pass safely under the bridge and on its way.

Bill's athletic career started at the age of sixteen with Belgrave Harriers when he ran mainly cross-country. Following his spear throwing antics during our primitive camping weekends the inevitable progression was made to the javelin which became his main event. He threw the javelin 170 ft (52 metres) and was club champion in 1961, 1962 and 1971.

He also represented Belgrave in the discus, shot, hammer and pole vault scoring many valuable points for the club, to which he became devoted. As a veteran Bill ran two London Marathons, beating the 3.5 hour mark.

Bill served the club in many ways for many years and was very proud when he was made Club President in 1975. More recently he was elected General Secretary. His services to Belgrave will be greatly missed.

Bill always put tremendous effort into all he did. He was fiercely proud of Tina and David and he was devoted to his grand children Jack and Katie with whom he loved to be with.

Bill took it very badly when Gwen died nearly five years ago. They were such a close and devoted couple and Gwen had always supported him in all his endeavours. Bill was always very heavy on emotional feelings. Bob Taylor, whose close friendship with Bill stretches from the fifties, was again very supportive in the period that followed as he started rebuilding his life.

Most people will be aware of Bill's recent cycling exploits. Reaching a very high standard, he remained as competitive as ever. It is not possible to name all the people who meant so much to Bill – there were so many! Bill was always considerate of those around him. He was extremely conscientious in his work as with all that he attempted. His standards were very high. He had great strength of character. He could, when required, be strong and firm but he had a very kind, soft centre. He commanded respect because he earned it. Bill expected little but gave everything. He was a perfect friend.

George Flanagan

Bill CouzensBillJimGwenGeorge StanleyPamSylviaLonnie DoneganTinaDavidJohnJenny MartinTonyStephanieDon MacLeanJackKatieBob TaylorGeorge FlanaganRon Linstead

Obituary · page 24

Stan Davies (1914-2002)

Gordon Biscoe

Obituaries

Stan Davies (1914-2002)

It is with much sadness that we have to announce the death of Stan Davies, who died on the 25th September 2002 from a heart attack, aged 88 years.

Stan was born in Leamington Spa on 13 October 1914 but while still a young lad the family moved to Nutley in Sussex when his father took a job as head groomsman on a country estate in Ashdown Forest. It was here that his running career really began as he ran a mile or so across the fields, back and forth to school each day. He also played football for his school and youth teams, as well winning several trophies for boxing.

After leaving school his early career was 'in service'. In 1938 he married Joan, with son David being born in 1939, to be followed later by daughters Susan and Elizabeth.

With the outbreak of the Second World War Stan soon found himself in the Army and as a member of the Service Corps, was one of those who helped drive the enemy out of the occupied island of Guernsey. After the war he became a Bank of England Messenger and ran for their sports club as well as Belgrave.

Like most of his contemporaries of that era, Stan ran road, cross country and track throughout the year. He was a key member of our teams and achievements at that time. He came 73rd in the National Cross Country Championships and held three consecutive Surrey Road Relay medals, a feat which he believed had not been achieved by others. He was also pleased to recall how he got the better of Tom Carter on several occasions. In the fifties he was Track Captain and was later made Vice President of Belgrave.

Stan was one of the true gentlemen of Belgrave, never pushing himself or his training methods forward but was always willing to help and spend time with anyone, particularly young runners, who sought his advice. I myself can recall many Saturday afternoon runs with him over The Common and Richmond Park just after joining the Club.

Quiet man Stan might have been, but he certainly had his moments. On one occasion while training on Tooting Bec Common he heard screams and saw a man molesting a young woman. On seeing Stan approach the man shouted that he had a gun, and ran off. Stan, not wishing to tangle with him at close quarters gave chase and literally ran the man 'into the ground' before he finally collapsed exhausted and gave himself up. He was duly handed over to the police and Stan was subsequently commended by Balham magistrates for his actions, although he never did receive any word of thanks or appreciation from the girl or her family.

On another occasion after a hard training run on Wimbledon Common and anxious to get back to the Hall for a good shower, he managed to stop a runaway horse, but was then left wondering what to do with it!

His dedication to Belgrave was never in doubt. Once, in hospital with a poisoned tooth, he calmly informed the doctors that he "had to be out by Saturday in order to run the London to Brighton Relay", the first time he had been selected. Much to his disappointment he was duly told to stay put!

After retiring, Stan and Joan moved to Newmarket, Joan's home town. This gave him the ideal opportunity to indulge in his other life long hobby of horse racing, no doubt strongly influenced from his early days in Nutley. He was very widely read on the history of horses; horse racing and jockeys, and would often 'pop in for a flutter' at the bookmakers, while out shopping.

Like his family, we will treasure the memories of Stan, and his efforts on behalf of the Club, and offer to Joan, David, Susan, and Elizabeth our sincere condolences.

Gordon Biscoe

Obituary · page 25

Bert Stroud 1932-2002

Obituaries

Bert Stroud 1932-2002

A quiet, thoughtful man, often laughing and quite unable to take offence at any of the dressing room banter, Bert Stroud will be sorely missed up at the Hall. He died on Thursday 14th November 2002 in his 71st year and had enjoyed nearly 38 years of Belgrave membership.

Bert was born in Fulham on 8th June 1932 but later moved to Merton where he attended Rutlish School. Leading an active life from an early age, he was always keen on cycling and walking and was a member of the Wimbledon group of the Youth Hostels Association from the mid-1950s. It was on a group visit to Winchester YHA that Bert shared fellow group member Margaret's soup and so began an enduring partnership. Before long they were married and both became life-members of the YHA.

Working in the insurance business, Bert was respected by all who knew him in the numerous threads of his life. He was active with many groups including Crisis FareShare - a body who obtain food near its "sell-by" date and distribute it to centres for the homeless and under-privileged; Critical Mass - described as a movement of bicycles ; Concern Worldwide - whose aim is to help absolutely poor people achieve major improvements in their lifestyles; Amnesty International - the voluntary human rights movement for whom he carried out administrative work. Bert was a caring man who did a lot of good things, quietly.

Music was another passion - all sorts - folk, jazz and classical - and at the age of 60 he took up playing the guitar.

Bert joined Belgrave Harriers in January 1965, introduced by Mike Blackwell, and soon became a regular in club and league cross country and road races. He never had any pretensions about becoming a star, plugging along gamely in the back third of the field, but as he aged an unusual phenomenon began to manifest itself: he just didn't seem to slow down! With each new veteran age group that he entered, his talent shone brighter and his lean, angular frame was seen loping along closer and closer to the front of the field. Eventually, in 1994, in the 60-65 age group, Bert was a member of our winning team in the BVAF National Cross Country Champs.

The onset of Bert's illness was quick. He was walking with his club mates on Exmoor back in May and enjoying his folk music and dancing. In November, three days before his death, he went into Richmond Park for the last time, by wheelchair, and entered the Princess Alice Hospice the day before he died.

Our condolences go to Bert's wife Margaret who shared his many interests, including membership of Belgrave Harriers, and to their sons Kevin, Mark and John.

Bert StroudBertMargaretMike BlackwellKevinMarkJohn

Obituary · page 25

Brian Nott 1934-2003

Sadly we must also report that Brian Nott, a regular Belgravian from the '60s but who was actually first-claim for Ranelagh Harriers, died in February this year.

He was employed by the Westminster Bank and on the surface seemed the archetypical bank clerk but on 12-Stage Relay days - held until the mid 60s over the London to Brighton course - he came into his own during the wild post-race celebrations at the infamous Black Swan where his repertoire of doubtful ditties was hard to match. We can see him now standing on a table with the late Don McLean and leading the assembled throng ... "My... father was the keeper of the Eddystone Lighthouse ..."

Brian travelled with Belgrave on the trip to Germany in 1964. Unable to obtain the required time off work - he simply took it. Two weeks later he turned up at work to find that he had still got a job, just, but was posted to a branch in north London.

Persistent foot trouble stopped Brian from putting together enough good training to realise his potential but he showed good form during the 1957-58 season when he beat an unfit Gordon Pirie - a fact he delighted in reminding us of for many years after. In 1962 he beat 2 hours for 20 miles and the following year completed the Polytechnic Marathon in 2:39:49. Twelve months later he improved this to 2:34:55 but his best ever performance came in 1965 when he was 8th Briton home over the Windsor to Chiswick course in 2:27:56.

Brian NottBrianDon McLeanGordon Pirie

Report · page 25

Quick-thinking Fred saves drowning toddler.

It's some time ago now, August 2001 to be precise, but we must record the valiant action of Fred Riach.

Enjoying his Monday afternoon jog through Battersea Park, Fred Riach was galvanised into action when he heard a splash and saw a two year-old girl struggling face down in the lake. While the girl's mother screamed hysterically Fred leapt into the water to save the choking youngster. Within minutes the drama had a happy ending as the spluttering child was restored to its mother. "I was just in the right place at the right time," said Fred, "I didn't have time to think - I just plunged in." Fred's colleague Gareth saw it another way: "Fred's quick-thinking saved the girl's life. He's a hero."

Fred RiachFredGareth

Notice · page 25

Tommy Green Cup - Dwain Chambers and Mick Jones

This year the jury was split and the trophy was awarded jointly to two men. Dwain Chambers has proved that he is a big time athlete. Six times he came up against Maurice Greene, at that time the World Record holder, and five times the Belgrave man came out on top. Had Dwain not lost to the USA's Tim Montgomery in that World Record breaking race at the IAAF GP Final, then he would have been officially rated no. 1 in the world. As it is, he is clearly no. 2, having equalled the European and UK 100m Records with 9.87. For his European Championship win Dwain is ONE of the men whose name will be engraved on the Tommy Green Cup for 2002.

The citation on the cup reads "for the most meritorious performance of the year". And performances don't come any more meritorious than those set by the man who has aptly been described as one of the most popular men in our sport - hammer man Mick Jones. Mick has been an international since 1984; he has competed in World, European, Commonwealth and Olympic Games. Five times he has been AAA Champion. In 1998 he came home from Kuala Lumpur as silver medallist; but in 2002 he capped a marvellous career by winning the Commonwealth Games Hammer Throw and for that performance Mick was jointly awarded the Tommy Green Cup.

Dwain ChambersDwainMick JonesMickMaurice GreeneTim Montgomery

Obituary · page 25

Jean Hutcherson 1925-2003

We regret to announce the death of long time member Jean Hutcherson at the age of 78. Jean spent nearly all her life living in Wimbledon. On leaving school she was advised by her parents to apply to study at Pitmans College to equip herself as a secretary. But Jean was having none of that and took a job at the Wimbledon store Elys - until she realised that it was secretarial work she preferred and, having made the decision herself, did indeed study at Pitmans.

Her career took her to the British Broadcasting Corporation where she was involved in the administration for the children's programme Blue Peter. Later she became secretary to David Coleman, the athletics commentator, and travelled with the BBC team to the Olympic Games. Her interest in athletics was born.

Jean was a member of Selsonia Ladies Athletic Club until that club disbanded. She then joined Belgrave Harriers in 1983. Always closely associated with our fledgling women's section, she became our Officials Secretary and was regularly to be found beavering away in the old caravan at Battersea Park, working out the results at Thameside League and home Women's Southern League matches. She very often travelled to away fixtures with the women's teams where she willingly became part of our field events judging team.

Jean was very much involved with the Surrey County Athletic Association and had been Secretary to one of that organisation's forerunners the Surrey County Women's AAA from 1973 to 1990 and their President in 1985.

Jean HutchersonJeanDavid Coleman

The Centenary Cup - Vernicha James

Vernicha James set senior club records at 100m, 200m and 400m in 2002. She represented Britain in the Spar European Cup Superleague, won the Commonwealth Games 200m trial and ran in the bronze medal winning 4x100m team in Manchester. In the World Junior Championships she collected a unique set of medals - bronze in the 4x400m, silver in the 4x100m - and gold in the individual 200m. And for that World Championship win in an outstanding 22.93, Vernicha was awarded the Centenary Cup for the best performance of the year.

Obituary · page 26

Arthur Whitehead 1913-2003

C. Dabbs

Obituaries

Arthur Whitehead 1913-2003

Arthur Whitehead was born in Hindhead, Surrey, on 10th April 1913. He attended King's College (Wimbledon) Preparatory School and then Sedbergh School in Cumbria. On leaving school, he entered the legal profession and qualified as a solicitor in 1937 before the outbreak of the Second World War.

During the war, Arthur saw active army service for several years, and as a captain in the Black Watch Regiment partook in the Allied Invasion of Europe in June 1944. Shortly after the Normandy landings he was horrifically wounded and was hospitalised for several months. He was subsequently discharged from the Army on account of these injuries. (In addition to severe shoulder and arm injuries, Arthur was left with a permanently damaged right hand - which necessitated him to learn to write with his left hand - and a 50% reduction in capacity of one of his lungs.)

In the Spring of 1945, Arthur moved into Lauriston Cottage, adjacent to his stepmother's home, Lauriston House on Southside, Wimbledon Common. By coincidence, in the Autumn of 1945, Arthur noticed the regular gathering of runners on the Common just outside his cottage. These runners were, of course, Belgrave Harriers, and Arthur decided that perhaps gentle running might assist the recovery from his war injuries. Arthur duly "signed up" and his long association with Belgrave had begun!

Arthur's initial outings were in the company of Joe Deakin (gold medallist 3 miles team race 1908 Olympics!) and Joe's young grandson Joe Cleverly, both members of Surrey A.C. (It should be remembered that during the war years and just after many running clubs had no facilities whatsoever - but Belgrave Hall remained open to runners of all clubs during this period.)

Arthur described his training group as an "odd trio equally handicapped by old age, youth and injury" but they ran regularly from the Hall and at Tooting Bec track. As Arthur's injuries improved, so he advanced to the Belgrave "slow pack" (led by George Still) and then further to the "medium pack" (led by, among others, "Champ" Smith).

Arthur's training programme was augmented by regular early morning runs across the Common for a brief swim in Queensmere. Further outings were made by Arthur's running group on evenings when the Hall was closed by utilising the sitting room and bathroom of his cottage. Group running from Lauriston Cottage had begun!

By now, Arthur had started racing in both club and inter-club cross country races and he ventured further when he competed in long distance road races in 1949. There were few long road races at that time, but among them were the Mitcham 15, Wigmore 15, Finchley 20, Surrey 20, Kent 20, Chichester to Portsmouth 16 and, of course, the Windsor to Chiswick marathon. Arthur and his road running group (now comprising Denis Brickwood, Geoff Pearson, George Chantler, Wally Wortly, Arthur Penstone, Bob Plummer and Joe MacDonald among others) ran all these races over the next few years.

Arthur's best performances all came in 1953 when he recorded the following times:

Surrey 20 miles - 2 hrs 06 mins Windsor to Chiswick Marathon - 2 hrs 49 mins London to Brighton - 6 hrs 44 mins (in 15th place)

As a matter of interest, Jim Peters won the above Windsor to Chiswick marathon in the then world's best time of 2 hrs 18 mins 40.2 secs.

At this time, interest in road running was increasing and in 1952 the Road Runners Club was formed. The main instigators were Ernest Neville and John Jewell and Arthur was elected to the initial committee. The RRC had an enormous influence on the expansion of road running in the UK and still functions to this day. For many years, the RRC newsletter was typed on stencils and then duplicated on an old "Roneo" machine before being enveloped and posted to several hundred RRC members.

The year 1952 also saw the "birth" of the first Belgrave 20 with Arthur as the organising secretary. It proved very popular and was described as "a first class organisation" by Athletics Weekly. The race, of course, continued for some 40 years, before dwindling interest and increasing traffic problems caused its closure.

In 1954, Arthur organised the first Lauriston Christmas Paarlauf ("paired relay"). The suggestion for the race came from Charlie Smart who had enjoyed such races while serving in the Control Commission in Germany. The race proved very popular (especially with hot punch and mince pies afterwards!) and continues to the present time. Christmas 2003 will see the 50th running of the race now organised by Lauriston Runners Club.

By 1956, an increasing number of runners were using Arthur's accommodation at Lauriston Cottage. Not only runners for Belgrave but from other local clubs too - Ranelagh, Surrey A.C., S.L.H., H.H.H., Mitcham, Poly, Wimbledon A.C. and Hercules A.C. amongst them. On regular occasions, the cottage became the temporary home for visiting South African teams participating in the annual London to Brighton road race. At times, the cottage was full to the rafters!

However, following the death of Arthur's step-grandmother in 1958, Lauriston House was sold for re-development but Arthur retained his cottage and acquired the cottages adjoining his own. The rear of the property was developed with a proper changing room, showers and toilets in 1962, and further enhancements have been made over the years. Furthermore, to bring some measure of control over the runners using the facilities (sometimes between 50 and 60 on Sunday mornings) Lauriston Runners Club was set up in 1966. Although members have contributed financially by way of subscriptions and donations over the years, the vast majority of the financing came from Arthur. His generosity was extended further when in the early 1990s the freehold of the running quarters of Lauriston Runners Club was donated to the Club. The Club continues as before - open seven days a week - with membership being open to runners of all clubs.

(It gave Arthur particular pleasure to see the success of runners using the facilities ... Olympic, European, Commonwealth and National Champions together with world record holders.)

Over many years, it was a regular practice of Arthur to organise long weekends or holiday weeks in the Yorkshire fells and Lakeland fells. Accommodation was either under canvas or in a rented cottage. Daily outings (or "rounds" as Arthur described them) of 20-30 miles in the tough terrain were the plan. Arthur was in his element in the hills with extraordinary strong legs and breathtaking speed downhill. He was particularly pleased with one "round" consisting of a route round the three mountains in the lakes - Scafel Pike, Helvellyn and Skiddaw - a distance in excess of 50 miles, achieved by a small group in 1959.

Although, Arthur's racing activities ceased in the mid-1950s, he decided to have a "final fling" in 1980 some 25 years after his last race. Thus, in the over 65s National Cross Country Championship at Wolverhampton in March 1980, Arthur emerged as National Champion, at the age of 66. (The following day, a congratulatory telegram arrived from Steve Ovett cautioning Arthur "not to talk to the press!")

Arthur retired from his legal practice in the early 1970s, but continued to run almost daily until his late 70s.

In 1989 he left Lauriston Cottage to live in the town of his old school in Sedbergh, Cumbria. He continued with less strenuous "rounds" in the local fells, but in latter years had to content himself with gentle walks.

Sadly in March 2003, Arthur sustained a severe stroke and passed away on 24th April 2003, just two weeks after attaining his 90th birthday.

Arthur's passing will be mourned by vast numbers of runners and officials not only in the UK but overseas as well. He will be remembered for his kind hospitality, great generosity and wise counsel but, above all, for his courage and determination in overcoming his war injuries. We bid farewell to Arthur Whitehead - a runner and a gentleman.

C. Dabbs

The Editor wishes to thank those who have gone to the trouble of taking photographs at many of our track, road and cross country fixtures - in particular Rex Bale and Leo Coy. Letter · page 27

Adelaide Calling - an Old Belgravian makes contact

David Mitchell

"Watching the Commonwealth Games on TV my mind went back to the 50's and early 60's when I wore the claret and gold. In this nostalgic mood I trawled the net and found your excellent website.

I was saddened to read of the passing of Bill Couzens, who was a contemporary. I was an 880 man and he was a spear chucker but we ran as juniors in the Middlesex cross country championships with Derek Crookes, George Flanagan and Jimmy Radford under the supervision of Bob Taylor, a miler and half miler of some distinction before illness cut his career short. In the late 50's into the early 60's I co-edited the Belgravian with Clive Shippen and was a committee member under such people as Alf Harley, Bill Lucas, Tom Carter and Arthur Penny.

After University and marriage I emigrated to Australia and severed my links with the Club which has clearly become a successful and highly competitive outfit. Congratulations to you all.

Any contacts you can make for me would be much appreciated. As memory joggers I went to Sloane Grammar School and Queen Mary College, won the boys, youth and junior medal competitions at the club, trained at Battersea and Hurlingham, won the All England Schools 880 in 1958 but never did much as a senior.

I would love to hear from anyone around at that sort of time."

Regards, David Mitchell e-mail: [e-mail withheld]

Report · page 27

Frank Ward and the "Blackstaffe" are back.

In the dark days of the winter of 2001-2002 our Cross Country Team Manager scoured London in an effort to find the whereabouts of our two long lost cross-country trophies - the "Macher" Shield and the "Blackstaffe" Shield. The former was found in the cupboard under the stairs in the home of a previous winner. But all hope was given up of ever recovering the prize for our 7 mile Championship from 1910 until the early 1990s - the "Blackstaffe".

However, one summer Saturday, while driving down to Worthing for a Women's Southern League match, Bill Laws spotted a likely looking runner springing gazelle-like along the A24. And didn't this runner have have a very familiar style... Bill pulled over and waited ... and sure enough it was exactly the man he thought it was: Frank Ward, a champion in our races several times and at one time our Hon. Treasurer. We have not had any idea of his whereabouts for nigh on a decade now.

Frank immediately agreed to come along to his local Worthing track later in the afternoon and there he met up with any number of Belgravians who were delighted to see him again. Tea at the Ward household followed and there Frank announced that he would have great pleasure in handing over to our CC Team Manager a certain trophy that had been lying under his bed these 10 years past - The "Blackstaffe Shield". Frank sends his kind regards to all those who remember him and hopes very much to meet up with them at some future function.

Frank WardFrankBill LawsBill

Feature · page 44

Maureen Knight piles up the points

As we put together this magazine and type up the results we cannot help but be impressed by the huge contribution made towards our points scores by Maureen Knight. Javelin, shot, hurdles, jumps - the more technical the event the better she seems to perform. Derek Jones has records of who has done what in our women's team going right back to the first days of our women's section. Maybe in our next issue we'll try to bring you a Women's Southern League points table along the lines of the Man's BAL list produced by Bill Laws.

Maureen KnightDerek JonesBill LawsRex Bale

What's on at Belgrave Hall

Saturday 28 June 2003 - Race Night/Cocktail Evening. This evening is now well established as the place to have a flutter! Cocktails and Pimms as usual. The summer event - don't miss it. Cost - £6, including buffet. (Cocktails, bar and bets extra!) Tickets available from the Social Committee - reserve yours now by phoning Helen ([phone number withheld]) or Nina ([phone number withheld]) now. Don't forget to sponsor a horse as well - even if you can't make it on the night, this is a great way to support the evening, with a chance to win a bottle of wine.

Saturday 4 October 2003 - Quiz Night. Always a popular night and your chance to team up and put your general knowledge skills to the test. Traditional fish and chip supper. Cost - to be confirmed to include supper. Tickets available later this year.

Saturday 22 November 2003 - Annual Dinner Dance. At the Reigate Manor Hotel. Accommodation will be available - full details to follow later in the year.

Saturday 6 December 2003 - Darts Championship. This was a great success last year and is great fun for both the serious and not-so-serious darts players. Hot snacks will be available as usual. Small entry cost for darts on the evening. Watch out for further publicity later on this year.

Report · page 56

They've done it again! National 12-Stage title retained

AAA Men's 12-Stage Road Relay, Sutton Park, Sutton Coldfield, 26 April 2003

With five new faces in the team an outsider might have been forgiven for thinking that the Bels were vulnerable. But as we've said before, our strength is in our tremendous depth and when one analyses the times it can be seen that the result is not down to outstanding performances by a few men - it's out and out solid running right the way through the squad with less than a minute covering the short stage men and not much more covering all the long 'uns. Those five new guys took their chances and did us proud. It was the first time in a decade that a club had returned to Sutton Park to successfully defend their title - and when you think how tight it was in 2001 (we lost by six seconds) we were oh! so close to pulling off a hat trick. Now when was the last time a club managed that ... In fact Tipton had four in a row 1989-92 and they had a treble before that; and then back in the '50s and '60s South London H, Derby and Coventry Godiva had some huge sequences. But seriously, let's take it as it comes, enjoy the present and remember how lucky we are that all this has come together during "our time".

Stage 1. Following our recent custom, the previous year's last stage man was moved onto stage one and Paul Freary enjoyed the company around him to bring us home just 46 seconds off the lead, getting pipped on the line by Salford to give us 10th. Birchfield had opened up strongly with Mike Openshaw and no less than five of the top ten long stage times were set on this initial leg. Newham & Essex had a disaster as Ian Grime obviously had a big problem and could manage no more than 52nd place.

1 Birchfield 26:01; 2 Liverpool 26:03; 3 Chester-le-Street 26:06; 10 Belgrave 26:47. Fastest: 1 M.Openshaw (Birchfield) 26:01; 2 A.O'Connor (Liverpool) 26:03; 3 M.Scaife (Chester-le-Street) 26:06; 10 P.Freary (Belgrave) 26:47.

Stage 2. Now for some fireworks! Maria Sharp had no sooner finished her opening stage for our women's team than she was tearing back down the course to urge on her Stephen. Stephen Sharp has been in cracking form and we expected him to move us very close to the lead. He did all that was expected - a fine run - but funnily enough was 3 seconds slower than 2002 and we would have put money on him breaking 14 minutes. As the afternoon wore on Stephen's time looked better and better, for only three men managed that elusive sub-14 standard, two of them down the field on this stage. We were now just 14 seconds adrift of Birchfield while Tipton and Morpeth were half a minute or so behind but inside the top ten.

1 Birchfield (0) 40:44; 2 Belgrave (+8) 40:58; 3 Sale HM (+3) 41:08. Fastest: 1 C.Davies (Telford) 13:50; 2 C.Livesey (Preston) 13:52; 3 S.Sharp 14:11.

Stage 3. Last year Kevin Nash had to pull out of the team with a knee problem. No such bad luck this time as Kevin confirmed his fine run in the Southern event and moved the Bels a couple of seconds closer to Birchfield. Behind it was all-change as Stewy Bell moved Chester-le-Street into the frame. Warmby brought Newham up 18 places but the biggest rise of the day came from fastest man on the stage, Farrow, who brought Derby up 23 places to 28th. It was Nasher's best run on this course in our colours - but again, strangely, 30 seconds down on what we might have expected.

1 Birchfield (0) 1:07:46; 2 Belgrave (0) 1:07:58; 3 Chester-le-Street (+3) 1:08:51. Fastest: 1 K.Farrow (Derby) 26:13; 2 M.Warmby (Newham &EB) 26:22; 3 M.Skinner (Blackheath) 26:46; 7 K.Nash (Belgrave) 27:00.

Stage 4. The animal was let loose on stage four and by the time the hill had been reached he was snapping and snarling at the heels of the "Stag" ahead of him. David Anderson had told us he would be very disappointed with anything outside of 13:50 and he certainly went off at a pace that promised to deliver such a time. Soon he was opening up a Belgrave lead that was never to be lost but as he ran out through the finishing funnel he was kicking himself and muttering - "I went off too fast". More importantly, he'd turned a 12 second deficit into an advantage of over a minute.

1 Belgrave (+1) 1:22:15; 2 Birchfield (-1) 1:23:20; 3 Notts (+2) 1:23:44. Fastest: 1 D.Anderson (Belgrave) 14:17; 2 J.Rogers (Sale HM) 14:41; 3 S.Burton (Notts) 14:45.

Stage 5. There was only one point in the race that had us worrying a little and with stage five we'd reached that point. Allen Graffin's knee problem is now behind him and recent sessions suggested that he was well on his way back. But fearful of letting the team down Allen did feel that it was prudent to have another man warming up for stage five and we'd make a decision as to who would step out onto the road ten minutes beforehand. Reserve Roger Alsop duly went through his pre-race ritual but when it came to it, Allen was fine. He certainly admitted to feeling his lack of fitness at the end of his stage and was a minute down on his 2002 fastest of the day - but was still our second fastest man. Newham were working their way back from the brink, now in 14th, but Mark Morgan had moved Tipton into a medal position and Birchfield were still going great guns in second.

1 Belgrave (0) 1:48:59; 2 Birchfield (0) 1:49:47; 3 Tipton (+3) 1:51:12. Fastest: 1 J.Moorhouse (Birchfield) 26:27; 2 Allen Graffin (Belgrave) 26:44; 3 M.Morgan (Tipton) 26:48.

Stage 6. A few weeks ago we weighed up whether Jonathan Blackledge should be an 'A' runner at Milton Keynes. He grasped that opportunity and showed that he should be considered for the big one at Sutton Park. He got his wish and by golly didn't he run well to take us to the halfway point. Can you really believe that just one year ago this young man was running in the mini marathon on London Marathon day - and here he was at the AAA Senior Men's 12-Stage Road Relay, taking over in the lead from Allen Graffin, spending a mere 14:40 out on the road with only the lead motorbike to chase and increasing our advantage by a minute to hand on to Aussie international Martin Dent. Jonathan's time stood up as 8th fastest of the day and surely showed UKA (if they noticed) that they should have selected him for the Junior World Cross.

1 Belgrave (0) 2:03:39; 2 Birchfield (0) 2:05:25; 3 Tipton (0) 2:06:32. Fastest: 1 C.Thompson (Aldershot F&D) 13:47; 2 J.Blackledge (Belgrave) 14:40; 3 A.Fulford (Swindon) 14:48.

Stage 7. Our lead stretched out to 2 mins 34 secs as Martin Dent ran in his first AAA Championship and took 48 seconds out of Birchfield's Rob Birchall. It had been a busy week for Martin as he only arrived back in London from Paris at 5:30pm the previous evening. He was our fastest long stage of the day and now sets his sights on a forward placing at Balmoral in a couple of weeks time. McCarthy of London Irish set the fastest long stage time of the day - the only man to go under 26 minutes - and the first of the Hudspith brothers began Morpeth's big push. But it was Tipton who made the most impact as Danny Robinson closed to within seven seconds of the silver medals.

1 Belgrave (0) 2:29:58; 2 Birchfield (0) 2:32:32; 3 Tipton (0) 2:32:39. Fastest: 1 M.McCarthy (London I) 25:48; 2 D.Robinson (Tipton) 26:07; 3 W.Burns (Salford) 26:18; 4 M.Dent (Belgrave) 26:19.

Stage 8. Usually enjoying an earlier leg, this time it was Richard Ward's turn to move back down the order. He clocked fastest for the stage and our lead reached its maximum of 2 mins 45 secs but again, it was one of those stages where we would have all thought he'd be half a minute quicker. Maybe those three races in three days the previous weekend - including a fell race - had something to do with it! Birchfield were still hanging in there with an 18 second margin over the green and white clad Tipton men.

1 Belgrave (0) 2:44:46; 2 Birchfield (0) 2:47:31; 3 Tipton (0) 2:47:49. Fastest: 1 R.Ward (Belgrave) 14:48; 2 R.Russell (Thames V) 14:58; 3 R.Green (Birchfield) 14:59.

Stage 9. As always Paul Evans received a huge reception as stepped onto the road. Our double Olympian and Chicago Marathon winner enjoys a great deal of respect from the road running fraternity. Paul had not been at his fittest in recent months, putting in time to study for a diploma in sports massage therapy, but a time trial over 5 miles the previous weekend earned him his place in our dozen. Tipton were now coming on strong with Nick Jones taking them to within four seconds of Birchfield while Mark Hudspith ran fastest on the lap for Morpeth, still in fourth.

1 Belgrave (0) 3:12:25; 2 Birchfield (0) 3:14:31; 3 Tipton (0) 3:14:35. Fastest: 1 M.Hudspith (Morpeth) 26:31; 2 G.Raven (Sale HM) 26:40; 3 N.Jones (Tipton) 26:46; 9 P.Evans (Belgrave) 27:39.

Stage 10. Will Cockerell has been reserve for us at Sutton Park on several occasions and this time, in spite of having run London two weeks earlier, he made the cut and fully justified his selection by running second fastest on the lap. Flogging himself up that final stretch his legs were "gone" and there wasn't an ounce of effort left in him as he lay panting on his back having sent Al Stewart on his way. The Tipton surge was on! They'd gone into second place through Cotton who ran fastest on the stage and they had two very good men to come - but they had 1 min 55 secs to make up.

1 Belgrave (0) 3:27:30; 2 Tipton (+1) 3:29:25; 3 Birchfield (-1) 3:30:13. Fastest: 1 S.Cotton (Tipton) 14:50; 2 W.Cockerell (Belgrave) 15:05; 3 S.Dudley (Notts) 15:23.

Stage 11. Al Stewart tells us that he has run more times around the Sutton Park course than the rest of the team put together. He certainly loves the route which is kinder to the stronger runners as opposed to the speed merchants. Tipton must have been hopeful of narrowing the gap and maybe even doing a "2001" on us on the last stage as they now turned out Stuart Hall, a sub 50 ten miler, and still had Nigel Stirk to come. But Al chose his 21st run in the Park to clock a personal best and far from losing ground ran fastest on the lap to open the gap to two minutes and more.

1 Belgrave (0) 3:54:23; 2 Tipton (0) 3:56:29; 3 Birchfield (0) 3:57:38. Fastest: 1 A.Stewart 26:53; 2 S.Hall (Tipton) 27:04; 3 A.Morgan-Lee (Salford) 27:20.

Stage 12. ... And we still had Adam Zawadski revved up and raring to go! Better known as an 800/1500 runner in the 1:50/3:42 region, Adam obviously has a lot of talent to show us at the longer distances as well. Another who had to run a time trial to work his way into the team when illness kept him out of the Milton Keynes line-up, Adam squeezed one more second away from the Tipton boys for another fastest stage and 6th fastest of the day. The last three stages had seen Belgrave and Tipton topping the fastest circuit lists.

And so the race was ours and the season closed on another high. Many thanks to all those who supported us here at Sutton Park and at the many events throughout the winter. You - and we - all know who you are and we love you for it. As for the Team Manager - well, he tells us that there couldn't be a finer bunch of guys to work with.

1 Belgrave (0) 4:08:55; 2 Tipton (0) 4:11:02; 3 Birchfield (0) 4:12:49; 4 Morpeth 4:13:32; 5 Salford (0) 4:15:05; 6 Sale HM (0) 4:18:05; 64 teams started; 57 teams finished. Fastest: 1 A.Zawadski (Belgrave) 14:32; 2 N.Stirk (Tipton) 14:33; 3 D.Swinburne (Morpeth) 14:59; 4 A.Renfree (Shaftesbury B) 15:03; 5 M.Boulstridge (Birchfield) 15:11; 6 D.Mason (Salford) 15:29.

Fastest legs of the day Short stage (2.995 miles): 1 C.Thompson (Aldershot F&D) 13:47; 2 C.Davies (Telford) 13:50; 3 C.Livesey (Preston) 13:52; 4 S.Sharp (Belgrave) 14:11; 5 D.Anderson (Belgrave) 14:17; 6 A.Zawadski (Belgrave) 14:32; 8 J.Blackledge 14:40; =14 R.Ward 14:48; =34 W.Cockerell 15:05. Long stage (5.380 miles): 1 M.McCarthy (London I) 25:48; 2 M.Openshaw (Birchfield) 26:01; 3 A.O'Connor (Liverpool) 26:03; 4 M.Scaife (Chester-le-Street) 26:06; =5 N.Goodliffe (Holmfirth) & D.Robinson (Tipton) 26:07; 10 M.Dent 26:19; 19 Allen Graffin 26:44; =22 P.Freary 26:47; =28 A.Stewart 26:53; 35 K.Nash 27:00; 68 P.Evans 27:39.

Report · page 57

Belles exceed expectations in National 6-Stage

Catherine Eastham

AAA 6-Stage Road Relay, Sutton Park, Sutton Coldfield, 26 April 2003

Having listened to the men's pre-race pep talk that morning, the ladies were glad not to be facing that level of pressure just yet. Still, pressure of a different sort came when two of the team, Juliette and Helen S, hadn't arrived by the time the team needed to be declared and couldn't be contacted on their mobiles. A team restructure was needed, which may have actually benefited us in the end. Although our runners' times (except Maria's) were slower than predictions, due to the strong wind, the team's final position of 11th was 4 places better than predicted. So it was a good day for the Belles.

Stage 1 After a strong run in the Southerns on the anchor leg, Maria Sharp wanted to reverse the order and lead off, figuring that starting up with the main pack would pull her around to a faster time. And that it did, for her 9th place finish in 17.01 gave the Belles their fastest leg of the day, and she beat her own previous best time for the course and won her bet with the men's first leg man, Paul Freary, as to who would finish higher up the field! Maria's run placed her as 18th fastest overall on the day.

1 Chester-le-Street 16:20; 2 Wirral 16:38; 3 Swansea 16:41; 9 Belgrave 17:01. Fastest: 1 D.Heneghan (Chester-le-Street) 16:20; 2 H.Lawrence (Wirral) 16:38; 3 C.Dugdale (Swansea) 16:41; 9 M.Sharp (Belgrave) 17:01.

Stage 2 As the first of three of the Belles marathon team to go out on the course there was interest in whether Louise Cooper would find the extra training beneficial or whether the marathon would have tired her out. It seemed the wind was the biggest problem though, and by two and a half miles Louise began to find it a struggle. Battling to the end though, Louise brought the team through in 17th place, roughly as expected.

1 Salford (+5) 33:45; 2 Swansea (+1) 33:53; 3 Sale HM (+2) 34:06; 17 Belgrave (-8) 36:50. Fastest: 1 R.Jones (Salford) 16:54; 2 R.Goddard (Swansea) 17:12; =3 D.Riding (Sale HM) and C.Martin (Telford) 17:17; 23 L.Cooper (Belgrave) 19:49.

Stage 3 Helen Alsop, team captain and running in her first National road relay was nervous, but confident that she wouldn't lose many places over the course. In fact, she maintained the team's position with a 19.19 leg which made her our third fastest of the day. Again though, the wind and the hilly course made it a tougher run than expected. Meanwhile, Sale Harriers were beginning to pull away at the front.

1 Sale HM (+2) 50:43; 2 Salford (-1) 51:57; 3 Swansea (-1) 52:11; 17 Belgrave (0). Fastest: 1 J.Heath (Sale HM) 16:37; 2 J.Craft (Headington) 16:46; 3 S.Murphy (Shaftesbury B) 17:07; 17 H.Alsop (Belgrave) 19:19.

Stage 4 Angela Walker was the second of the marathon team to head off down the considerably shorter 2.995 mile course. Her task was to pull up one or two places and bring the Belles nearer to their top 15 target. Angela went one better and pulled up 3 places, with the Belles moving into 14th. The top three places hadn't changed, but Sale's lead had extended to 1 min 15 secs.

1 Sale HM (0) 1:07:36; 2 Salford (0) 1:08:18; 3 Swansea (0) 1:09:45; 14 Belgrave (+3) 1:15:35. Fastest: B.Jenkins (Salford) 16:21; 2 A.Waterlow (Sale HM) 16:53; 3 R.Brown (Chester-le-Street) 16:58; 10 A.Walker (Belgrave) 19:26.

Stage 5 In only her second outing for the Belles, Helen Smethurst awaited the start of her leg with nervous anticipation, having never competed in a road relay before. With the other team members reporting back on how tough the course was, how tough would Helen S be and could she maintain the team's position? Finishing as the Belle's second fastest of the day, Helen looks set to be a regular feature in forthcoming fixtures and brought the team back in a very respectable 11th position. Sale's lead had extended to nearly 4 minutes but WSE&H had pulled into 3rd with a strong leg from Paula Fudge.

1 Sale HM (0) 1:24:47; 2 Salford (0) 1:28:30; 3 Windsor SE&H (+3) 1:28:57; 11 Belgrave (+3) 1:34:44. Fastest: 1 L.McCreesh (Sale HM) 17:11; 2 P.Fudge (Windsor SE&H) 17:35; 3 I.Robertson (Headington) 17:40; 8 H.Smethurst (Belgrave) 19:09.

Stage 6 The final leg saw Juliette Clark taking over for the Belles. Although not in the same form as this time last year we knew that she wouldn't succumb to the pressure of being anchor leg. By holding on with a solid 19.28 run Juliette enabled the Belles to hold on to that 11th place, surpassing expectations by 4 places. Given that the Belles didn't have the talents of Birhan Dagne, Getenesh Tamirat, Catherine Berry and Anne Hegvold on the team, one can only wait in anticipation for future results...

1 Sale HM (0) 1:41:00; 2 Windsor SE&H (+1) 1:44:56; 3 Chester-le-Street (+1) 1:45:32; 11 Belgrave (0) 1:54:12. Fastest: 1 H.Yelling (Windsor SE&H) 15:59; 2 K.Gillibrand (Sale HM) 16:13; 3 M.McDonnell (Chester-le-Street) 16:31; 13 J.Clark (Belgrave) 19:28.

Catherine EasthamJulietteHelen SMariaMaria SharpPaul FrearyD.HeneghanH.LawrenceC.DugdaleM.SharpLouise CooperLouiseR.JonesR.GoddardD.RidingC.MartinL.CooperHelen AlsopJ.HeathJ.CraftS.MurphyH.AlsopAngela WalkerAngelaB.JenkinsA.WaterlowR.BrownA.WalkerHelen SmethurstHelenPaula FudgeL.McCreeshP.FudgeI.RobertsonH.SmethurstJuliette ClarkBirhan DagneGetenesh TamiratCatherine BerryAnne HegvoldH.YellingK.GillibrandM.McDonnellJ.Clark

Photograph · page 58

Relay Runners

Above (1): Belgrave's men retained the AAA 6-Stage Road Relay Championship at Sutton Park in October 2002. Mark Miles, Richard Ward, Spencer Barden, Stephen Sharp, Allen Graffin and David Anderson celebrate keeping hold of the trophy for another year. This is the hardest team title to win - can they make it three in a row in October 2003?

Left (2): She's here - she's there - she's everywhere! One of Belgrave's keenest supporters and racers, Maria Sharp has been the cornerstone of our women's road relay teams. And we're pretty sure it won't be long before she's taking a team medal home with her.

Below left (3): Louise Cooper had a great run in the SEAA 6-Stage Relay at Milton Keynes, in spite of her main aim being the London Marathon.

Right (4): Birhan Dagne was 5th fastest of the day in the AAA 6-Stage Relay of 2002 and remains our club record holder for the Sutton Park circuit. She's been preoccupied in 2003 with the arrival of a beautiful baby boy who arrived at St. Thomas' Hospital on 27 March. His name is Emanuel and he weighed in at five and a half pounds.

Photos 1, 2 and 4 by Mark Shearman, 3 by Patricia Mead.

Photograph · page 58

National 12-Stage Champions 2002

Opposite page. Belgrave's awesome AAA 12-Stage Relay team of 2002 won by a record margin of over six minutes - a turn around from the previous year's loss by a mere six seconds. It was the first time the club had won the "National" 12-Stage since 1951 when the race was organised on an invitation basis over the London to Brighton course. It also meant that at the end of the 2001-2002 season both National road relay titles (6-stage and 12-stage) were held by Belgrave - and the club went on to retain both in the 2002-2003 season. Top left: Spencer Barden took the Bels into a lead on stage 3 that they were never to lose. Top centre: At the end of his stage Stephen Sharp had clocked the second fastest short stage of the day - second only to our own Andy Graffin - but was later pushed down to third fastest by another Belgravian, David Anderson. Top right: Richard Ward came into the team as reserve on the Friday and proceeded to demonstrate that he should have been in the dozen in the first place! Centre right: "Belgrave's double whammy". Allen Graffin and Andy Graffin clocked the fastest long and short stages of the day. Bottom left: Mark Miles took the Belgrave lead to over two minutes on stage 5. Bottom centre: ... And Allen Graffin extended it to three and a half minutes on stage 7. Bottom right: A regular on stage 11, Al Stewart was five minutes clear but still ran a personal best. Back page: An ecstatic Paul Freary celebrates an outstanding team victory as he breaks the tape. All pictures by Mark Shearman.

Spencer BardenStephen SharpAndy GraffinDavid AndersonRichard WardAllen GraffinMark MilesAl StewartPaul FrearyMark Shearman
